It has been amended once by a provision more specifically regulating the application of the previous Code until the new Juvenile Penal Code is in force. The Government has decided to submit a second, more extensive proposal for amendments and to supplement the Smurf Code after a good two years of application, in particular due to some systemic comments, due to warnings and proposals from the competent authorities on the detection, prosecution and infringement of the application of the Code, and due to the need to harmonise individual legal provisions, in particular in the specific part of the Code, with amended grounds for the legal order of the European Union and binding international legal acts which the Republic of Slovenia is obliged to enforce in an appropriate manner in its criminal law regime. Thus, in the general part of the Code, the Government proposes to regulate compulsory psychiatric treatment, to create an appropriate institute of negligence and extreme force, to facilitate the imposition of monetary penalties in the daily amount, to coordinate the imposition of penalties for offences in the course of the course of the course, to minor property damage in the event of a moving thing, to alter the changes required on the basis of changes in the specific work and so on. In particular, it proposes introducing new or amending and supplementing existing crimes against budgetary and public financial resources and against the safety of public road transport, harmonising with the legal acts of the European Union and other relevant international instruments, amending and supplementing the relevant legal descriptions of offences found in theory and practice and so on. The changes to the Smurf Code were urgent and welcome. In the Group of Members, we also really agree with the Justice Minister, Mr Zalar, that the Penal Code is changing thoughtfully. Therefore, we also welcomed the establishment of the Criminal Law Council as the consultative body of the Minister of Justice, composed of respected legal experts, theorists and practitioners in this area. The Council was set up on 12 January 2009 and its task is to exchange and gain views on rules and policies in the field of criminal law and to adopt appropriate positions and guidelines for their preparation. Unlike former Minister Sturm, the Minister decided to prepare the changes with greater assistance from the profession. As you may recall, in his previous term, Minister Šturm has indeed commissioned professional research to prepare a novel of the 1995 Code, but experts at the Ljubljana Department of Criminal Law carried out research and collected material on the basis of which to prepare a novel simply ignored. Even more; two of them, under a commitment of silence with one of the current constitutional judges, participated in a group that secretly and quickly prepared for us a controversial code with a life sentence. We were therefore expecting to replace the Smurf Code in the Group of Members, but this does not mean that we would do so by risking the possibility that we would accept it overnight like the previous government. In the parliamentary group, we even thought about stopping the implementation of the Smurf Code at the beginning of the mandate, allowing the professions to write it again. But we haven't come any further than conversation. That's why we're a little bit disappointed today, at least to some extent. We are in the parliamentary group and today we advocate the writing of a new code, for which there are professional bases in the form of a study of necessary changes prepared at the Faculty of Law in Ljubljana. Virtually all the authors of the study are also members of the above - mentioned world. There was a lot of talk in coalition coordination on the code. But it has prevailed to think that a completely new code requires more time, that it is only a novel and that the novel does not make sense to go in the direction of accounting with former Minister Sturm and the authors of his code. That is why the Penal Code also leaves a prison for life. In the Group of Members, we really believe that the introduction of life imprisonment is one of the biggest mistakes if I express myself very mildly, the Smurf Code. It is still difficult to accept that the drafting of the Code has given more thought to the possibility of a referendum than to the objectives and commitments of the left-wing coalition or the Government on human rights and fundamental civilisation values. When this coalition announced in the 2008 elections what it would change if it came to power, it was not the fear of a referendum. In addition, this coalition with the Minister of the Interior, who, in order to implement the Constitutional Court's decisions, has been able to regulate one of the major marks of our independence, a status which has been deleted, which has resulted in both referendum requirements and parliamentary investigations. And she survived quite a few referendums. We regret that it appears that some points of the coalition contract will remain only on paper and that we have not been able to contribute to a more human society. The abolition of the death penalty in the field of punishment would be a real symbolic step for us. In coalition coordination, we also drew attention to the fact that we have avoided the amendment of the Penal Code in order to fully address the contentious changes and general arrangements of crimes against honour and good name. In our opinion, it is frightening that we still have a series of crimes in the Penal Code, which incriminate also the journalistic reporting that Sturm's Penal Code has even extended to media editors. So anyone, including a journalist, can be punished even with a prison sentence. Moreover, Article 166 of the Penal Code concerning the media extends the scope of criminal liability to the editor. The editor is supposed to represent a filter in press releases, and he is supposed to predict in advance whether any reporting constitutes a crime or not, and he has also prevented it. So the editor can also answer criminally with a prison sentence by publishing a journalist who has not prevented it. In our view, such an arrangement does not meet the requirements of a democratic society. We are aware that, with the proposed addition in Article 166(1), you are further limiting the responsibility of the responsible editor for the offences committed by public publication. Nevertheless, we believe that closing journalists is fundamentally contrary to the fundamental principles of democratic society. Freedom of expression is the foundation of a democratic society and independent media are the guardians of all three branches of government. Therefore, punishing the editors for the offences referred to in Articles 158 to 166 of the Code, which are committed by public disclosure of these acts, is not in accordance with the current degree of democracy in our society. Since we have recorded an increasing number of criminal proceedings against journalists in Slovenia since the adoption of the new Penal Code, I believe that law enforcement issues should also be addressed, even if they are introduced on a proposal. The question is whether this is even in line with the current degree of democracy of our country. In the criminal proceedings of politicians and other officials, it is a feeling that it is a country against a journalist. And that is unfair, because in the face of insulting judgments about their work or position, unlike other citizens who file a private action, pay judicial fees and lawyers, they enjoy the protection of the state. The problem is, in fact, the very structure of the criminal procedure. If the state prosecutor is authorized to prosecute, the police are automatically involved, you receive calls to journalists for information, requests for initiation of investigative procedures and a variety of other things, which negatively affects media freedom. And finally, when a journalist comes to court, the prosecutor represents the state against the media. We therefore propose that the prosecution of offences referred to in Articles 158 to 162 and 166 of this Code be abolished against a State authority or a municipal or provincial authority or against an official or military person acting on an official duty or on a proposal. We ask ourselves repeatedly why Slovenia is falling, losing its seat on the press freedom charts. We believe that this is not exactly the democratic solution introduced by the Smurf Code, which unfortunately now continues in the current Penal Code. Since these proposals have not been included in the amendment of the Penal Code, we will propose amendments to the provisions of the Code, which, in my opinion, revive media freedom, intimidate journalists and create conditions for self-censorship in the Zares Group. In the Parliamentary Group, we would also like to draw attention to the amended Article 148 of the Code, which provides for the sanctioning of unjustified use and possession of high-prison work. Unjustifiable use and possession of copyright only equates to the crime against property referred to in Article 204 [[Thieves]] which speaks of alienating the physical moving thing. We stress that the actions mentioned are not entirely comparable or the same as for the victims and the actual material situation. In doing so, we would stress that, in similar cases, from Germany, Italy and France, their criminal legislation penalises copying, reproduction, public advertising, crucifixion and illegal exports and imports. [[...]] In doing so, we must point out that existing legislation already covers the prosecution and punishment of all those using peer-to-peer, Torrent or similar online file exchange programmes, with a severe prison sentence. In the event of the use of the software mentioned above, there is also a dispute, which is dealt with in the existing 149th Article as unjustified reproduction, making available to the public or renting. Such drastic punishment will not help to protect copyright in the Republic of Slovenia, but the change will disproportionately incriminate large groups of people in the Republic of Slovenia, which could also place a heavy burden on the justice system when consistently applied. Therefore, in the second reading, we will propose to balance Article 148. Despite these comments, we will support the amendment of the Code.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-14-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u164	Thank you very much for your words, Mr. Vice President. A respected choir! Any interference and modification of the Penal Code means an extremely difficult and responsible task for the National Assembly as legislator. It is one of the most important laws of all society, especially of a democratic state, and it is also a difficult matter, which needs to be given a little more attention in the introduction. Why did the government decide to prepare such an extensive novel after two years of use of KZ-1? Due to the systemic comments submitted by a large part of Slovenian criminal law theory already in the preparation and implementation of the 2008 KZ-1. On the basis of warnings, initiatives and proposals by the courts, the State Prosecutor's Office and other competent authorities regarding the inconsistencies and shortcomings detected in the use of KZ-1 in law enforcement and prosecution bodies. Warnings have been given regarding specific provisions that have led to problems in cases of excessive persecution following the annulment of a legal decision, of offences of abuse of position or trust in economic activities, of damage to foreign rights, of obstruction of road traffic and others. In view of the need to harmonise individual legal provisions, in particular in the specific part of the Code, with amended grounds for the legal order of the European Union and with binding international legal acts which the Republic of Slovenia is obliged to enforce in an appropriate manner in its criminal law regime. In doing so, I must point out that we have been critical in the Parliamentary Group of Social Democrats in adopting the new KZ-1 in 2008, and we have warned that there will be difficulties in implementing the law. Unfortunately, we were right back then. The proposed amendment to the Penal Code follows the following important principles and objectives. These are: the principle of humanity, in particular with the possibility of adapting the daily amounts to the specific circumstances of the perpetrator, with the possibility of immediately replacing the prison with house imprisonment and work in general favour, which is also possible as proposed in the event of a fine. The same basic basis also gives rise to a proposal to reduce the duration of psychiatric treatment and shorten the time limits for monitoring it compared to those currently in force. In the framework described, the principle of individualisation of criminal sanctions also arises. These principles follow the objectives of providing a criminal legal basis for compulsory psychiatric treatment of non-contributory and substantially reduced perpetrators of criminal offences and thus filling the legal void that has occurred since, at the same time as the new Penal Code, no special law has been adopted to regulate coercive measures of a medical nature. In order to facilitate the introduction of the criminal pleading institutes and the plea agreement with the possibility of a wider limitation of the penalty, a conditional judgement and a court notice, and to simplify the decision on the replacement of the sentence and the financial penalty, and to enable the courts to determine the daily amounts of the fine in relation to the general estate situation of the accused by clearly distinguishing the dose of that amount from the determination of the daily amounts. The principle of subjective criminal liability shall be taken into account in the redrafting of the provisions on extreme force and actual error, the provisions on intent and negligence, the voluntary inadvertent derogation from the offence and the establishment of the subsidiary responsibility of the responsible editors for offences against honour and good name. Malpractice shall be defined in a way that will not only bind the findings to the expected or foreseeable consequences, while maintaining the established criminal form of conscious and unconscious negligence, at the same time adapting the provision on the eventual intent accordingly. A more appropriate distinction should be made between criminal offences and offences — the principle of strict legality in criminal substantive law referred to in Article 28 of the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia and the meaningfully equal principle set out in Article 7 of the European Convention on Human Rights. This principle is of particular importance in view of the determination of individual offences in the law, which led the applicant to a clearer description of the signs of those offences which could be concealed by offences; these are: public order and peace, road transport, weapons, personal data, and the same as with regard to the distinction between criminal acts. Let me also mention the changes and additions to crimes against the economy, the new offence of damaging public funds and the re-enactment of offences against road safety. However, an important novelty in our criminal code will be the identification of new grounds for alleviating punishment. The planned amendments and additions to the Criminal Procedure Act introduce the possibility of a decision on the basis of the confession of the accused or the plea agreement between the State Prosecutor and the accused into the Slovenian legal order. In accordance with comparable rules, in such cases it would be possible to mitigate the penalty or sentence of a second penalty with broader, more favourable options than those applicable to the type and level of the penalty. In such cases, the principle of prudence may have full effect only if the applicant in the application and the court in the case of a sanction is not bound by the same restrictions as laid down by law in cases where the relief is permissible under a specific provision, for example in the case of an attempt, aid, legal error, in cases where the penalty may be dismissed and so on, or where special relief circumstances are provided. Therefore, the proposer proposes, for cases of recognition or agreement, the establishment of a different minimum prison sentence to be imposed on such an offender. The limits are placed in such a way that a permissible easing on average per third is determined by the minimum penalty, which is also applicable to the sentence of monetary penalties instead of imprisonment. This will give sufficient scope to negotiate with the scapegoats in cases where the law enforcement authorities assess that this is necessary and possible because of the particular interest in preventing and detecting dangerous crimes. If a particular interest is given to the disclosure of a particular criminal activity, the penalties of the perpetrator involved with the law enforcement authorities may be reduced accordingly. In doing so, the applicant took into account that the current criminal policy, which already applies the existing options to the legal borders, also dictates that additional levels of easing be extended, since, for example, 85% of all prison sentences were imposed on a conditional basis and only 15% were effective in 2009. Without further restraints in the legal framework, there would not be sufficient scope for negotiations and agreements with defendants. The principle of equality, which must also apply to the use of new institutes, is provided in the new Law on the State Prosecutor's Office, which allows for a more precise direction of the conduct of state prosecutors in individual specific cases with general instructions from the heads of state prosecutors or the Attorney General's Office, including with regard to proposals for punishment in cases of recognition or agreement. Thus, in the context of the requirement for equal treatment, a comparable sanctioning of similar cases may be provided, taking into account the principle of individualisation of penalties. It will also be appropriate to ensure the position of the accused by a procedural provision that, before accepting recognition, the court must, in particular, assess whether the accused has understood the nature and consequences of the recognition given, whether the recognition has been given voluntarily and whether the recognition is clear and complete and supported by other evidence in the file. If the conditions are not met, the court will refuse recognition. The same relief provisions are also proposed in the case of conditional conviction and in the case of a court notice. This allows for such sanctions to be imposed at broader borders in cases specifically justified by the recognition of guilt or of an agreement with the accused, as provided for in the general provisions of the Code. The Penal Code, which we are considering today and which we consider appropriate in the Group of Social Democrats for further consideration, represents an important step in the development of criminal legislation. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-14-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u173	Thank you. Mr. Kolaric has the floor. Get ready for Kek, Klasinc or Mr Barovic.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-14-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u174	Thank you very much for saying this, Mr Vice President. Mr. Minister with colleagues, colleagues, colleagues! In his opening remarks on the most important objectives of the proposed amendment to the Penal Code, the Minister pointed out that it also includes the creation of a clearer basis for the successful treatment of economic crime. To date, implementation of the 2008 Code shows that there has been a missed opportunity for more appropriate legislation, which is essential to remedy and correct as soon as possible, and I think we are correcting this by this law with KZ-1B. In this respect, all proposed changes to the legal descriptions of individual crimes against the economy are positive. In particular with regard to the abuse of the situation and rights, bankruptcy and greater protection of public funds. It is important to address the serious shortcomings of the current KZ-1 text, which does not define the concept of economic activity as an object of criminal law protection. The need for such a definition stems from the predominant assessment of the theory and practice that without the precise and sufficiently broad content of this concept, which is not defined in the current law of 2008 at all, there can no longer be a successful prosecution of economic crime on the ground. The concept of economic activity in the specific part of KZ-1 is used in various versions as a sign in the case of twelve offences and it is already not understandable that the code for the purposes of criminal law has not defined it, as was the case under the previous law. The removal of the definition of the importance of economic activity has created additional doubts and uncertainties and, in particular, in certain border areas, has enabled perpetrators of abuses, corruption and other dangerous and harmful acts to avoid prosecution and conviction. It is also unacceptable that it is systemic, as it is contrary to the principle of legality, whereby crimes must be committed in advance and determined as precisely as possible. The proposed definition is modern and is not solely based on the criterion of the entity’s business activity – for example the production and transport of goods, the provision of services on the market, banking and other financial operations – but on its general nature. It is defined as a profitable activity. The definition thus does not only cover classical economic operators, but also all other legal entities, where this is an activity carried out on the basis of supply and demand on the market and against payment. The proposal also provides for the re-implementation of the concept of economic activity as an activity for which payment is agreed or prescribed. This makes it possible to punish abuses in the field of non-economic, public services, concessions, civil law, which are not directly linked to entrepreneurship, and in these areas serious illegal acts may also be committed with a view to achieving private benefit, in particular in the context of budgetary or public financing. An extended definition will allow for acts committed in these areas, or against such perpetrators, the use of economic crime if the activity is carried out professionally or in an organised manner, i.e. if it is not an occasional and one-off work for a smaller payment. The definition of the former law is also in line with the requirements of the internationally accepted obligations of the Republic of Slovenia in the field of corruption, which we have not followed so far. The recommendations of the OECD Working Group on Anti-Corruption in the field of non-profit tasks, where opportunities for active and passive bribery are strongly exposed. This should be added to the fact that the draft amendment now clearly defines the concept of economic activity in all its emerging forms – implementation, management, decision-making, representation, management and supervision. This concept includes the management of property, movable property, money and so on, other forms of financial assets and the resources of legal persons of public and private law, the availability and supervision of these assets. According to practice, this property is often the subject of serious crimes. Thus, the proposed law provides for criminal protection in the field of management of all public funds, public finances, public property, irrespective of the organisational form in which it is located, in the case of abuses or other illegal acts committed in the manner set out in the chapter against the economy. The proposal for a Code therefore represents a major shift in the legal protection of public administration, as it provides a comprehensive and clear basis for punishing abuses, corruption and other acts that may occur in the management, management or supervision of public funds, as well as abuses of trust in private legal persons. I will, of course, support the bill. Thank you very much.

Seven. 2011 in the context of the votes. This concludes this agenda item. We're moving to 21. At the end of the day, this is firstly a proposal for a law on amendments and amendments to the law on criminal procedure. The draft law was tabled by the Government. With regard to this bill, the group of sixteen Members and Members of Parliament are the first-signed mags. Borut Saiovic demanded that the National Assembly hold a general debate. In order to further explain the proposal, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, the Minister of Justice, Mr Zalar.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-14-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u202	Your Honor! The main objective and content of the proposed amendments to the Criminal Procedure Act is to provide effective grounds for effective procedures. This law brings a number of important news. Thus, it is proposed to introduce a new interim phase of the procedure, the so-called pre-trial claim, on the legality of the indictment and before the call for a main hearing. On this claim, the defendant will, in the presence of the defence counsel and the state prosecutor, declare the offence and proceed with the proceedings. If he pleads guilty, a reduced procedure will be carried out, it is only an expression of sanction, without the main treatment being carried out. If he does not admit the guilt, but at this stage all possible inadmissible evidence will be eliminated, all procedural proposals by the parties will be decided, which facts are indisputable between the parties and which are disputed, which evidence will be determined. In short, it's going to be a proof and a timetable. This will allow for a significantly faster execution of everyone, I stress every criminal procedure. This novel also offers the possibility of an agreement, an agreement to move faster and an end to the main proceedings. Even in cases where the defendant does not confess, he will have the opportunity to decide on the so-called accelerated criminal procedure and to waive certain guarantees, such as: the deadline for the preparation of the defence, the composition of the court, the trial in the presence and so on. Then there's another big news, a plea agreement. This institute will legitimize the possibility that the prosecutor and the accused can agree on the conditions under which the accused will be found guilty. The possibility of a criminal trial based on a prior agreement between the Prosecutor and the Prosecutor has been accepted in many European countries and has produced very good results. We are promising of this, in particular from the point of view of the unloading of criminal justice with cases that do not need to be tried and extensive evidence at the main hearing. The law, of course, brings a number of other novelties, such as the abolition of an exceptional remedy of a requirement for an exceptional penalty reduction, because this objective can be achieved within existing institutions, or the redesign of the conditions for filing a claim for the protection of legality as another exceptional remedy. Then there are simplifications in international criminal matters, in particular the enforcement of a foreign judgment or the withdrawal of prosecution or extradition of an individual. This is indeed a significant change in this area, which has a direct impact on the duration or speed of criminal proceedings. Of course, this harmonisation is also necessary to a certain extent due to the changes in the Penal Code that you have already discussed since, inter alia, the need to reintroduce safety measures for compulsory psychiatric treatment and protection in the health institution and in the workplace. Then this amendment is necessary for another separate set of reasons, due to the decisions of the Constitutional Court, because this amendment fulfils the three decisions of the Constitutional Court relating to the Criminal Procedure Act. Moreover, there are then those changes where the case law has found that the individual provisions are not sufficiently clear and therefore necessarily require a recast. These amendments to the law are a result of in-depth professional work and I would like to stress that preparations for the amendment of this amendment to the Criminal Procedure Act have already begun in the previous term. We have continued with these preparations and with this broad professional coordination, these changes are now ready. This is why they are also supported by a very broad professional consensus in Slovenia. These provisions have virtually not experienced any mention of valid concerns, except one expressed by the information officer, and this has been done in the course of this legislative procedure and will provide an opportunity to clarify these open issues. I therefore suggest that you continue to support this law. Thank you.

The most important novelty concerns the introduction of an intermediate phase between the sole jurisdiction of the defendant and the call for a main hearing. This is the stage in which the defendant will plead guilty to the crime he has been charged with. If he pleads guilty, he will be punished without the main treatment. Without the main proceedings, the criminal procedure may also be concluded on the basis of an agreement concluded by the Prosecutor and the accused. However, if the defendant does not plead guilty, the substance of the dispute between the parties must be crystallized in the indictment proceedings, that is, which of the facts at issue will be dealt with at the main hearing and with which evidence to prove. The Member States of the DeSUS Group hope that this phase will be fully implemented in practice, as it will certainly contribute to a more efficient and speedy procedure of the courts, in the final phase of the application, by providing for the pardon of the penalty in case of admission of guilt to a more favourable so-called final solution or penalty for the accused himself. This, of course, reinforces the role of state prosecutors and, at the end of the process, only closer cooperation between the latter and the police in the investigation of the crime. In short, we are convinced that the proposal is a good one to justify the purpose for which the Ministry has undertaken these changes, so it will be supported by the DeSUS Members. ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-14-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u214	Thank you very much, Mr. Vice President. A respected choir! I am amending and supplementing the Penal Code as a rule followed by amendments to the procedural law, the Criminal Procedure Act, and so, too, in this case. In the Group of Social Democrats, we support the main solutions to the proposed amendments to this procedural law relating to the following areas. These are: to provide legal basis for a more effective course of criminal proceedings, in particular the regulation of the procedure by allowing for the implementation of reduced and simplified forms of criminal proceedings and the economic course of the main proceedings, the fulfilment or harmonisation in the three Constitutional Court decisions, the simplification and acceleration of international cooperation in criminal matters, the harmonisation with the Criminal Code and the Public Prosecutor's Act adopted two days ago, and the elimination in practice of the deficiencies identified in the applicable criminal procedure. In the position of the Group of Social Democrats, I would like to highlight the following important news. Provision of legal bases allowing shorter and simplified forms of criminal proceedings to be carried out and the economic course of the main proceedings. The new section of the pre-trial application provides for a new interim phase following the jurisdiction of the defendant and prior to the call for a main hearing. If the accused is found guilty, a reduced procedure shall be carried out, that is an expression of criminal sanctions without the main treatment, but if not, unlawful evidence shall be excluded at this stage and the parties' procedural proposals shall be decided, and it shall find out which facts are disputed between the parties and which evidence will be demonstrated at the main hearing that the main proceedings can be carried out faster and more economically. It will also be possible to incorporate other forms of reduced and simplified procedures into the procedure. As I have already mentioned in the Penal Code, this is an important novelty in the Criminal Procedure Act, which is the possibility of an agreement on the recognition of guilt. A very important novelty is presented in the provisions of the new chapter governing this plea agreement. These provisions give rise to the possibility for the Prosecutor and the accused to agree on the conditions under which the accused will be found guilty. The possibility of a criminal trial based on a prior agreement between the prosecutor and the accused is also accepted in many other European countries. A further proposed amendment is the abolition of the exceptional remedy of the requirement for an exceptional reduction of the penalty, as many other institutions allow and take into account the reasons why this exceptional remedy can be lodged and the establishment of a condition for the lodging of a request for the protection of legality, which is a preliminary exhaustion of legal remedies, both formal and substantive. We also support these changes. In view of the introduction of new institutes and the modification of the procedure under the jurisdiction of the accused, amendments to the relevant applicable articles relating to the main proceedings, the appeal procedure, the provisions on the expulsion of judges and the like are also proposed. Further innovation is to simplify and accelerate international cooperation on criminal matters. The amendments relate to all international legal aid institutions governed by the law. These are: small international assistance, enforcement of a foreign court decision, withdrawal of prosecution and extradition. The proposed amendments aim to simplify and accelerate cooperation on criminal matters, and the main changes concern, in particular, the admissibility of the granting of aid as prescribed by the procedures of the requesting State, the institution for the temporary surrender of the person, the structural changes in the provisions relating to the enforcement of a foreign judicial decision and the abolition of the threshold of the prescribed penalty in respect of the withdrawal of prosecution. It has already been pointed out that the amendment of the Law on Criminal Procedure is also in line with the Penal Code and the amendment of the Law on the State Prosecutor's Office. The new Law of the State Prosecutor's Office, the novel ZDT-B, has partly changed the organisation of the State Prosecutor's Office and therefore there is a need for harmonisation in several articles of the CAP. Coordination of the Criminal Procedure Act is also necessary due to planned changes and additions to the Penal Code, which will redefine the safety measures of compulsory psychiatric treatment and protection in the healthcare and liberty institutions. In the Parliamentary Group of Social Democrats, we would like to see a new, successful period of action for the competent authorities, on the one hand, to protect the victims and victims of crimes and, on the other hand, to successfully and swiftly end criminal proceedings and to adequately punish the perpetrators of crimes, following the implementation of all three laws — the State Prosecutor's Office, the Penal Code and the Criminal Procedure Act. This could lead to the acquisition of so-called traffic data by order of the investigating judge, who called whom there, when and how long he spoke, not only for a certain means of communication, for example, a certain phone, but also for communication equipment, which means the entire area of the selected mobile operator base station. Moreover, the requirement could also apply to multiple base stations, and could also refer to equipment providing other communication services such as e-mails and the global web. In doing so, we must not follow the argument that these needs are reasonable in order to increase the effectiveness of the investigations. The principle of proportionality for access of repressive authorities to personal data of citizens is gravely violated by the possibility of looking at data on all those who have been in reach in the area of a crime. In order to resolve these dilemmas, we will require, in the second reading, that information Commissioners be included in the working bodies of the National Assembly in the consideration of the amendments to Article 149.b. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-14-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u221	Thank you very much. Mr. Minister Aleš Zalar asked for the floor. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-07-14-SDZ5-Redna-30.ana.u222	Thank you very much, Mr. President. I would like to make some clarifications to Members and Members who are still insisting on this because there is some misunderstanding from the debates so far. First of all, with regard to possible abuses of the new institute of admission of guilt and negotiation between the prosecutor and the defendant. This institute, which someone has even said we do not need, has been known for a very long time in the European legal space and, of course, has its roots in the Anglo-Saxon legal system, but has gradually penetrated continental Europe and has been introduced by more and more countries in different forms. The scope of the Institute's use in practice has increased significantly since it was very effective because it has reduced procedures where possible and at the same time reduced the burden of prosecutors and courts with cases for which it is not necessary to carry out the whole evidence procedure with the main proceedings. In view of the wide use of this institution in the European legal area, the Council of Europe has issued even more recommendations in this regard concerning the functioning of criminal justice. Finally, let me mention the recommendations made in this regard by the European Prosecutors' Advisory Council and the recommendations made by the Consultative Council of European Judges. Both bodies are going in the same direction on this issue – strongly recommend to those Member States of the Council of Europe that have not yet done so to introduce this institution as soon as possible due to the good effects it has in practice. And Slovenia has fallen somewhat behind in this area in the past. It is high time that we followed modern trends and redesigned the criminal procedure in a way that would allow these procedures to be carried out, so-called simplified procedures or reduced procedures, which, of course, significantly burdens the criminal justice system. So it's not something that would have grown on the grassroots of strangers, it's even less about an arrangement in which prosecutors wouldn't be involved, or like someone said, "who was written by people who were never even on the D.A. and in the judiciary." That's not true at all. Look who was preparing this novel: former Chief Justice, current Chief Justice of the Criminal Department, Senior Judge, District Judge, Chief Prosecutor and Professional Officers of the Ministry of Justice. This was a narrower working group, and then, of course, an even wider range of experts participated in the assessment of individual institutes. They knew very well what they were up to. And interestingly, the process of preparing these changes was created during the previous government, and one part of this material was created during the previous government. We continue with this project. This is, of course, a profound intervention in our criminal procedure, we are aware of this, so this process has taken so much preparation, so much weighting of the possible versions of this institute. But we now know, in broad professional agreement, that this is what Slovenia needs right now. And if I sum up the reaction of the head of the state prosecutor's group on special matters: "Finally, that's what we've been waiting for." Those are her words. I fully trust our prosecutors, especially those in the most responsible positions, and I think our policy must also trust them. And I think it is wrong to try here by the public, which, of course, does not have such detailed knowledge of this area, conscious of some claims about prosecutors who feel otherwise. The Ministry of Justice has received strong support for amending the Criminal Procedure Act, both under the former Attorney General and under the current Attorney General. In this respect, there were no differences and there are no differences between them today. I don't know them. When there was a speech about the possible abuse of the admission of guilt institute. Look, why can't this abuse practically happen? Because there's always judicial control in the back. Nothing can happen without the court's approval. And the court, when assessing the appropriateness of such an agreement on the recognition of guilt, will always have to examine separately, and this is stated in the law: for the first time, whether the accused has understood the consequences of such recognition, for the second time, whether this recognition was voluntary, for the third time, whether this recognition is clear and complete to such an extent, because it is supported by all the other evidence that the Prosecutor must provide when entering such proceedings. However, even if such recognition had been given at an early stage of the proceedings, the court shall assess all the above and additionally: whether, at the time the prosecutor went to such an offer, sufficient evidence had been collected that he could have relied upon the existence of a reasonable suspicion that the accused had committed a specific crime and whether the accused had been informed of the description of the offence and whether the accused had been provided by a lawyer at that time, that is to say, a lawyer who must have, if not otherwise, had been officially liable. All these procedural guarantees are provided in the amendment to the Criminal Procedure Act so that this recognition institution cannot in any way be misused. This institution for the recognition of guilt and negotiation between the prosecution and defence has not been reintroduced by neighbouring Croatia from countries in the former Yugoslavia, which once had the same criminal procedure. And when I spoke to the Croatian Minister of Justice about the effects of this institution, he told me that they were extremely positive, that they were extremely positive, that the number of criminal proceedings that had been concluded in this way had increased significantly, that something else had even happened to them from this title – that they had increased the number of scapegoats, which then allowed them to pursue proceedings against other persons who had committed crimes and against whom the prosecutor had not had sufficient evidence. We therefore promise that, despite the fact that there is no legal tradition in Slovenia in the use of this institute, we will be able to experience similar effects when preparing a training programme for prosecutors and judges in Slovenia. We're already preparing this training. Our Centre for Justice Education is preparing this training, which starts early in the autumn and we are preparing it in cooperation with foreign experts, with practitioners, with those who already know and use these institutions in practice. It will therefore not be about training on foreign law, but it will be about tactics, a strategy, when negotiating, how much evidence, how to offer reduced penalties, how to deal with crimes, how to approach and how to deal with them. That's important news. We are aware that this will require some additional preparation, study, practical exercises if you want, especially with the prosecutors. But this process runs parallel to the process of enacting this law. In no case will this novelty be able to prolong criminal proceedings. This is excluded because, even if such an agreement is not reached, the pre-trial claim, which is a complete novelty and some kind of pre-trial claim to the main hearing, will clear up on this pre-trial claim all that is now being cleared through the entire criminal procedure when the expulsion of judges takes place at any hearing, the motion for the elimination of inadmissible evidence, the proposal for the implementation of new evidence. Even in final speeches, customers can, and often do so, propose the implementation of new evidence. And then the court, which intended to close the hearing, has to file a new hearing. From now on, this will be no longer due to the fact that at the very beginning of the proceedings, at the earliest stage, at one request, there will be only one decision on all these issues: whether there is any reason to appoint a judge, or whether there is any evidence that the defence claims to be inadmissible — if so, the court must decide — which facts are proven, which evidence will be drawn up, how long the proof will take. Then the whole plan of this procedure is made. We know exactly what it takes so many and so many hours and so many days to carry out this procedure. This means a large narrowing, a large concentration of the main treatment in each criminal procedure, I stress. This is the savings brought by the novel, whether or not the prosecutor will negotiate with the defendant regarding the admission of guilt. On this issue, whether the prosecutor will do it or not – this is his free judgment. This is the prosecutor's discretionary decision, and no one's forcing him to do that. No additional administration is involved. If the Prosecutor considers that it makes no sense to go into such a procedure, that he has such strong evidence that there is no such good defence against him, I do not know how good lawyers can challenge him, but he will not go into this procedure because he will promise a reliable conviction with an appropriate high sentence. Of course, the prosecutors have this judgment, and it's only right that we leave it to them. The comment was mentioned when, "you promised the financial police, and none of it is." That's not true. What is the National Investigation Office? What, though, are common investigative groups that prosecutors can engage? Financial experts, tax experts, experts in the field of the Anti-Money Laundering Office, the Securities Market Agency, what else is this? What, though, is the financial investigation brought about by the law on the confiscation of property of illegal origin? The financial investigation is a tool in the hands of the prosecutor. We have fulfilled what we have foretold. By the way, this 12th prosecution, which will carry out a financial investigation, will not only have 15 prosecutors, as mentioned, that the programme was one of the coalition partners, but will have 21, we have increased this number. Because the situation requires that prosecutors have sufficient capacity to combat economic crime. The question was raised as to why the investigation was still pending. It's gonna happen. Because this amendment to the Criminal Procedure Act is not the end of the reform. There's another part to follow. And this other part goes into full preparation in the fall. It's already done in one piece. In one part, it still needs to be upgraded, in particular from the stage of the main hearing and further complaints. However, I stress something — the abolition of the judicial investigation or the transfer of these powers to the prosecution — which will be a huge asset. This is not going to be a simple thing, not a financial thing, not a human resource, not a spatial one. For this, it will really take a very long time to prepare. This will not be a thing that can be done, for example, within six months of making such a change, by no means. And it will also go to the organisation of the courts and the prosecutions, to the human resources plans, to the financial resources, so we are realistic. We know what this country can afford in this area. You can afford what is before you today on sent-off benches. Article 149b He was mentioned by several Members and Members. Logically, because the information authority has specifically pointed out to it that it understands this provision of the law. And then, while the law was already in the legislative process, she just gave her opinion, and I would like to draw particular attention to that. We therefore have only from that moment forward its argument and will be able to face these views and these understandings in the next process. Now I can say the following. This novelty, brought by the novelty in Article 149b, second paragraph, does not extend the scope of the information that the police may obtain. Don't spread them. And now let me tell you why it doesn't spread. For the first time since the scope of such data is set out in paragraph 1 of this Article, not in the second. But the first one is unchanged. The first paragraph is unchanged. The range is the same. What does the second paragraph do — the one that changes and adds the word " equipment", not "assess" as someone said wrong? This second paragraph, however, sets out the mandatory components of the police proposal or the court order, i.e. the act as such. But this can only remain within the limits of the conditions laid down in paragraph 1. This hasn't changed a bit. And then: in any case, in any case, the proportionality of the proposed measure is decided by the court, never by the police themselves, never by the prosecution itself – the court. So we still have the most important guardian here — judicial judgment. And also, in these cases, of course, it's not about any wiretaps, it's about no wiretaps of content, it's just information, whether it was some kind of device, a specific phone, or whether it was at a certain point at a certain time. So that's the only subject of this order. Of course, there is no way of knowing which person it may have been. So, too, this fear is exaggerated. Nevertheless, we want to continue this procedure, in the direct exchange of views – not only with the information officer, but perhaps with someone else – to check whether we all understand in particular the first paragraph of this Article. If not, then it is better to establish an amendment in a way that prevents any ambiguous interpretation. But if we understand it the same way, of course, there is no need to amend this article. With that, I would finish my intervention and say just one more thing. The Criminal Procedure Act, as I have said, will still be subject to some changes, but it will not be possible, at least not in the manner presented here, to prohibit the repeal of the First Instance judgments. The constitutional profession and the criminal law profession have spoken about this several times. Here we have one limitation in our constitution, which, if we introduce this rule, means that our Supreme Court becomes in too many cases of Instant. In fact, we literally drown the Supreme Court in a mass of cases because it would become a court of appeal. That's the main problem. Despite the fact that we would like to have a regime where the repeal is an exception and not a rule, and I must say that in recent times this has changed and that there has been an increasing number of main hearings in the Court of Appeal. But in this way we collide with this provision of the Constitution and, of course, there are limits that we all know, after all, with the necessary two-thirds majority of the National Assembly. Thank you.
